//! Held change polls: JSON whitespace heartbeats, followed by one JSON result.
use super::{
    JsonBuffer, Shared, cors, lock, principal_of, query_params, refusal_json, respond_timed,
    trusted_dev_request,
};
use serde_json::{Value as Json, json};
use std::io;
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};
use tiny_http::Request;

const HEARTBEAT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(2);

pub(super) fn serve(request: Request, shared: &Shared, url: &str) {
    let params = query_params(url);
    let since: u64 = params.get("since").and_then(|s| s.parse().ok()).unwrap_or(0);
    let wait: u64 = super::wait_seconds(url);
    let began = Instant::now();
    let deadline = began + Duration::from_secs(wait);
    let paced = began + Duration::from_millis(100);
    let capability = match principal_of(&request, shared) {
        Ok(principal) => principal,
        Err(refusal) => {
            return respond_timed(request, shared, 401, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal) }));
        }
    };
    if let Err(denied) = crate::device_handshake::check(shared, crate::device_handshake::header(&request), false) {
        return respond_timed(request, shared, 409, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&denied) }));
    }
    let connected = &capability.0;
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#6-idle-live-clients-must-not-cost-a-thread — one principal's tabs cannot take the whole viewer pool; refused at once, never queued.
    let _share = if wait > 0 {
        match shared.long_waits.enter(connected) {
            Ok(share) => Some(share),
            Err(limit) => return super::long_waits::refuse_share(request, shared, limit),
        }
    } else { None };
    let present = capability.2 == snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full;
    let heartbeat = params.get("heartbeat").is_some_and(|s| s == "1") && request.http_version().0 == 1 && request.http_version().1 >= 1;
    let mut response = PollResponse { request: Some(request), started: false, shared };
    let initial = { let wake=lock(&shared.wake); (wake.seq,wake.eph.version.clone(),wake.generation) };
    let epoch = params.get("eph").and_then(|s| s.parse().ok()).unwrap_or(initial.1.epoch);
    let incarnation = params.get("ephi").cloned().unwrap_or(initial.1.incarnation);
    let interest: Option<std::collections::BTreeSet<String>> = params.get("ephTables").map(|s| {
        s.split(',').filter(|s|!s.is_empty()).map(str::to_owned).collect()
    });
    let wants_ephemeral = ["eph", "ephi", "ephTables"].iter().any(|k|params.contains_key(*k));
    {
        let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
        if let Err(why) = super::session_identity::persona_read_authority(response.request.as_ref().unwrap(), shared, &engine) {
            drop(engine); response.finish(401, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)})); return;
        }
        if present && let Err(error) = engine.presence_reconnected(&connected, shared.now()) {
            response.finish(500, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&error.refusal()) })); return;
        }
        if present { shared.presence.enter(&connected); }
    }
    let result = (|| -> Result<_, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
        // Entry always reads the store: the client may already know a head
        // committed after the last publication (LLP 3000 finding 5).
        let mut current=read_answer(shared, since)?;
        let mut observed=initial.0;
        let mut sequence_wake=false;
        let mut beat=Instant::now();
        loop {
            // The deadline is private scheduling state. Production polls wake
            // at it even without another application request; development
            // clock jumps run the same advancement through their control
            // request and notify this condition variable.
            let opening = if shared.personas {
                None
            } else {
                shared.next_time_audience()?
            };
            // Snapshot and wait share one mutex. Commit cannot pass between
            // the predicate check and the condvar's atomic unlock-and-wait.
            let wake=lock(&shared.wake);
            if wake.seq != observed {
                observed=wake.seq; sequence_wake=true; drop(wake);
                current=answer(shared, since)?;
                continue;
            }
            let tables=wake.eph.changed(epoch,Some(&incarnation),interest.as_ref());
            let reset=wake.eph.version.incarnation!=incarnation;
            let eph_ready=reset||!tables.is_empty();
            let durable=wake.generation != initial.2 || sequence_wake || current.0!=since || params.get("store_id").is_some_and(|id|Some(id)!=current.1.as_ref());
            let now=Instant::now();
            if durable || now>=deadline || eph_ready && now>=paced {
                let eph=snapback4_core::engine::ephemeral::Change {preserved:wake.eph.preserved_since(epoch,&incarnation),epoch:wake.eph.version.epoch,incarnation:wake.eph.version.incarnation.clone(),tables};
                let generation=wake.generation;
                // Replacement publishes its identity with the incarnation. No
                // engine lock is needed to finish an ephemeral-only wake.
                if reset {current.1=wake.store_id.clone();}
                return Ok((current,generation,eph));
            }
            if heartbeat && now>=beat {
                drop(wake); response.heartbeat().map_err(|e|snapback4_core::RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
                beat=Instant::now()+HEARTBEAT;continue;
            }
            let mut until=if heartbeat {deadline.min(beat)} else {deadline};
            if eph_ready {until=until.min(paced);}
            if let Some(opening) = opening {
                until = until.min(Instant::now() + shared.time_audience_delay(opening));
            }
            #[cfg(test)]
            super::test_work(&shared.poll_wait);
            let (wake, timeout)=shared.polls.wait_timeout(wake,until.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now())).unwrap_or_else(|e|e.into_inner());
            #[cfg(test)]
            super::test_work(&shared.poll_resumed);
            #[cfg(test)]
            if !timeout.timed_out() { super::test_work(&shared.poll_notified); }
            drop(wake);
            if timeout.timed_out()
                && opening.is_some_and(|at| shared.time_audience_delay(at).is_zero())
            {
                shared.advance_time_audiences()?;
                current = answer(shared, since)?;
            }
        }
    })();
    // @ref LLP 3000.033#1.2 — a reader never enters OR leaves presence.
    if present { shared.presence.leave(&connected); }
    // Recheck bearer liveness on a pooled detached reader. Ephemeral-only
    // wakes must not reacquire the serving engine merely to finish a poll.
    let auth_store_id = lock(&shared.wake).store_id.clone();
    let bearer = response.request.as_ref().unwrap().headers().iter().find(|h|h.field.equiv("Authorization")).and_then(|h|crate::auth::parse_bearer(h.value.as_str())).map(|token| token.trim().to_owned());
    let authenticated = match bearer.map(|token| (shared.reader(), token)) {
        None => Ok(()),
        Some((Ok((reader_epoch, reader)), token)) => {
            let resolved = match snapback4_core::identity::resolve_read_only_supervised(reader.store(),&token,snapback4_core::identity::ServiceSample { now: shared.now(), supervisor_revision: shared.worker_revision.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Acquire) },auth_store_id.as_deref()) {
                Ok(Some(current)) if current == capability => Ok(Ok(())),Ok(_)=>Ok(Err(snapback4_core::identity::diagnostics::refused(reader.store(), &token, shared.now(), trusted_dev_request(response.request.as_ref().unwrap(), shared)))),Err(e)=>Err(e),
            };
            match resolved {
                Ok(outcome) => { lock(&shared.readers).put(&reader_epoch, reader.into_inner()); outcome }
                // As for answers: a reader older than a finishing adoption is
                // not a verdict on the session. Ask again behind the engine.
                Err(_) => {
                    principal_of(response.request.as_ref().unwrap(),shared).and_then(|current| {
                        if current == capability { Ok(()) } else { Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "the poll credential changed")) }
                    })
                }
            }
        }
        Some((Err(_), _)) => {
            principal_of(response.request.as_ref().unwrap(),shared).and_then(|current| {
                if current == capability { Ok(()) } else { Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "the poll credential changed")) }
            })
        }
    };
    if let Err(refusal)=authenticated { response.finish(401,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&refusal)}));return; }
    match result {
        Ok(((seq,store_id,tables,preserved,changes),generation,ephemeral))=>{
            let mut reply=json!({"seq":seq,"tables":tables,"preserved":preserved,"changes":changes,"generation":generation,"store_id":store_id});
            if wants_ephemeral {reply["ephemeral"]=json!(ephemeral);}
            response.finish(200,reply);
        }
        Err(error)=>{let why=error.refusal();response.finish(if why.retryable {503}else{500},json!({"denied":refusal_json(&why)}));}
    }
}

type Checked = (u64, Option<String>, Vec<String>, Option<Vec<String>>, Json);
// @ref LLP 3000.036#6-idle-live-clients-must-not-cost-a-thread — one commit wakes every held poll; they share one answer per cursor.
/// Wake answers by (cursor, published head, generation, store identity). Only
/// a held poll woken by a publication asks here, and an answer is computed
/// after reading that publication, so its snapshot is newer than the poll's
/// entry and than anything its client knew when it asked. The answer names
/// no viewer, so polls from the same cursor share one read: a commit costs one
/// read per distinct cursor, not one per held poll. Entry and unheld polls
/// read the store themselves (the published counter is a wake signal, never
/// the source of truth). A shared answer is at most a second old and never
/// below the cursor; failures are not kept.
#[derive(Default)]
pub(crate) struct Answers { entries: std::collections::VecDeque<((u64, u64, u64, Option<String>), Instant, Checked)> }
const ANSWERS_KEPT: usize = 64;
const ANSWER_AGE: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);

fn answer(shared: &Shared, since: u64) -> Result<Checked, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
    let key = { let wake = lock(&shared.wake); (since, wake.seq, wake.generation, wake.store_id.clone()) };
    let mut answers = lock(&shared.change_answers);
    if let Some((_, _, hit)) = answers.entries.iter().find(|(k, at, hit)| *k == key && at.elapsed() < ANSWER_AGE && hit.0 >= since) {
        return Ok(hit.clone());
    }
    let checked = read_answer(shared, since)?;
    // Older publications are never asked for again.
    answers.entries.retain(|(k, at, _)| k.1 >= key.1 && *k != key && at.elapsed() < ANSWER_AGE);
    if answers.entries.len() >= ANSWERS_KEPT { answers.entries.pop_front(); }
    answers.entries.push_back((key, Instant::now(), checked.clone()));
    Ok(checked)
}

fn read_answer(shared: &Shared, since: u64) -> Result<Checked, snapback4_core::RuntimeError> {
    let _timing = super::latency::EngineSpan::new();
    #[cfg(test)]
    super::test_work(&shared.changes_refresh);
    if let Ok((epoch, mut reader)) = shared.reader() {
        // A pooled reader can predate an adoption that has committed but not
        // yet replaced the pool. Only a success is kept; anything else is read
        // again behind the engine, which waits for that adoption to finish.
        if let Ok(checked) = check(&mut reader, since) {
            lock(&shared.readers).put(&epoch, reader.into_inner());
            return Ok(checked);
        }
    }
    let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
    match shared.open_reader(&engine) {
        Ok(mut reader) => { drop(engine); check(&mut reader, since) }
        // A memory store has no detached reader: read under the engine.
        Err(_) => check(&mut engine, since),
    }
}

/// The notification's head, identity and invalidations share one read snapshot.
fn check(engine: &mut snapback4_core::engine::Engine, since: u64) -> Result<Checked, snapback4_core::error::RuntimeError,
> {
    let all_tables: Vec<_> = engine.schema().visible_tables().map(|(n,_)|n.clone()).collect();
    let schema = engine.schema().clone();
    let tx = engine.store_mut().read_transaction()?;
    let head = tx.next_seq()? - 1;
    let store_id = tx.optional_store_id()?;
    let changes = tx.commits_after(since, 500)?;
    // Advancing to the actual head must not skip invalidation beyond the
    // bounded commit page. Saturation conservatively invalidates every table.
    let mut tables = if changes.last().is_some_and(|(seq, _)| *seq < head) { all_tables }
        else { changes.into_iter().flat_map(|(_, tables)| tables).filter(|name| all_tables.contains(name)).collect() };
    tables.sort();
    tables.dedup();
    let preserved = tx.preserved_between(&schema, since, head);
    let changes = settlement_changes(tx.footprint_between(since, head), &schema, since, head);
    tx.rollback()?;
    Ok((head, store_id, tables, preserved, changes))
}

struct PollResponse<'a> {
    request: Option<Request>,
    started: bool,
    shared: &'a Shared,
}
      entry.resolve(outcome);
      emitWrite(outcome);
    }
    return true;
  }

  let flushing: Promise<void> | null = null;
  let retry: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | undefined;
  let retryMs = 250;
  let retryAfterMs = 0, retryOverload = false;
  function flush(): Promise<void> {
    if (flushing) return flushing;
    if (retry) { clearTimeout(retry); retry = undefined; }
    const run = async () => {
      try {
        while (!closed && !storeRefusal && pending.length > 0 && link !== "signed-out") {
          const entry = pending[0]!;
          sending = entry;
          let sent: boolean;
          try { sent = await send(entry); }
          finally { sending = null; }
          if (!sent) {
            if (closed || storeRefusal || !credentials) return;
            retry = setTimeout(() => { retry = undefined; void flush(); }, retryOverload ? jitter(Math.max(retryMs, retryAfterMs)) : Math.max(retryMs, retryAfterMs));
            retryAfterMs = 0; retryOverload = false;
            (retry as { unref?: () => void }).unref?.();
            retryMs = Math.min(retryMs * 2, 5000);
            return;
          }
          retryMs = 250;
          pending.shift();
        }
      } finally {
        // Release custody before this run resolves. A caller receiving the
        // last receipt may admit its successor before promise adoption ends.
        if (flushing === result) flushing = null;
      }
    };
    // A pending-list subscriber may submit another write at dispatch. Install
    // the flush owner before notifying it, so the same head is not sent twice.
    const result = Promise.resolve().then(run);
    flushing = result;
    void result.catch(() => {});
    return result;
  }

  const stopClock = clock.subscribe((shift, revised) => {
    // Sub-frame sampling noise must not make a query response refetch itself.
    if (closed || (!revised && Math.abs(shift) < 10)) return;
    sweepExpiries();
    scheduleRefresh();
  });
  if (!options.readOnly) delivery = onlineDelivery({ offline: () => setLink("offline"), url: base, fetch: doFetch, mint: mintId,
    ready: () => !closed && credentials && !storeRefusal && !refreshing && link === "online" && !!storeIdentity,
    current: () => !closed && credentials && !storeRefusal, headers: () => ({ ...headers }), revision: () => credentialRevision,
    floor: () => minimumReadSeq, store: () => storeIdentity, observe: (json, floor) => observeStore(json, floor, true),
    obsolete: obsoleteRefusal, refresh: () => refreshing, onRefused: options.onDeliveryRefused });
  void delivery?.start();
  const client: Client = {
    readOnly: options.readOnly ?? false,

test('unavailable staging timing suppresses header and stream-final clock changes',async()=>{
  let wall=1000;const clock=new ServerClock(()=>wall);const transport=clock.wrap(async()=>new Response('',{headers:{...headers(10000,12000),'x-snapback-session-timing':'unavailable'}}));
  const response=await transport(url);sampleChanges(response,{serverNow:10000,clockRevision:'host:1'});assert.equal(clock.issuedDeviceNow(),1000);
});

## 4. Staging clock

Wave-20 reporting amendment: LLP 3000.000.000.001 §10 changes the proof
advance return to `{now, fired, held}` and explains persisted boundary holds;
operator authority and clock execution semantics below are unchanged.

Only a store durably classified staging by deploy --staging may serve with
--clock-offset. Missing/unknown classification is production. This setting
is process-local, with the same signed-duration parser and monotone elapsed
clock as dev. No serve-time mechanism can reclassify an existing production store. Restart drops the offset unless explicitly
provided again; persisted timed-disclosure floors never retreat below their captured value.

Staging exposes the existing /dev/clock/status and /dev/clock/advance only
with any full service session: these are trusted operator credentials, as for
system jobs. Production refuses even with a service token, and an explicit
production serve offset refuses with E_CLOCK_DEV_ONLY before listener startup. Account, guest, read-only and unauthenticated
requests refuse. Recheck classification and session under the engine writer
before advancing. All fixture, reset, persona and dev-job doors remain closed.
Use the same advance implementation, schedule drain and ambiguous-reply law
as dev. CLI clock advance --release DIR mints its local service authority.
startServe accepts clockOffset and exposes now()/advance() through that
operator authority; it never changes store classification. Each helper control
mints a current staging-time operator session rather than caching a credential
across arbitrary time jumps. Ordinary user sessions retain normal expiry.

Hot release activation samples `shared.now()` under its engine writer, including
the persisted disclosure floor, and passes that one time through the core's
explicit `adopt_release_at`. A live preview probes the existing filesystem owner lock independently of
HTTP bind/address hints and acquires a stopped-store lock before taking its
SQLite snapshot, retaining that lock through inspection. A rollback preview
reuses its caller's existing ownership rather than probing its own lock as a
live host.
A live preview samples time through the same-owner,
store-bound existing private status protocol; no session is minted or clock
changed. A missing existing-store ownership sidecar refuses rather than assuming a
stopped host without exclusion; ordinary rollback preview can reestablish and
reuse its canonical ownership. A revision mismatch with the private database snapshot refuses as a
racing activation. Store contents and the clock are point-in-time samples, not
a freeze of later live writes. A stopped store's cold activation/preview uses
wall time, with the existing persisted disclosure floor applied by activation.
Positive and negative staging offsets are both covered by hot/preview tests.

## 5. Evidence
### 1.3 Read sessions: storage, minting, refresh and logout

**Storage fails closed.** Read sessions live in a **new table**,
`_read_sessions(token_digest, principal, kind, family, created_at,
expires_at, cap_expires_at, principal_generation)`. They are never rows in
`_sessions`, so:
- an older executable, which knows nothing of `_read_sessions`, cannot
  resolve a read token and refuses it as unauthenticated rather than
  serving it as a full session;
- no insert path can silently default a read session to full.

**Opening and adoption leave no committed changes on refusal.** Opening a
store is not permission to install auth schema before deciding whether its
backend can be used. A refused open or adoption preserves the schema (every
table, index and column), every stored row including `_meta`, and database
integrity. On SQLite the freelist count also stays unchanged. This is a
**committed-state guarantee, not whole-file byte identity**: physical bytes may
differ only in pre-existing free-page contents and journal or WAL residue.
SQLite deliberately [does not journal freelist leaf contents][free-pages], so a
DELETE-mode migration can reuse and overwrite those pages before rollback
returns them to the freelist. Allocated schema/data pages are restored. A large
WAL migration may spill dirty pages before a later refusal; rollback leaves
those frames uncommitted and invisible to readers but can retain the enlarged
file. Committed, uncheckpointed frames remain readable. Existing journal mode
is retained. Pre-admission SQLite connections disable checkpoint-on-close
before reading the database and retain that setting on refusal, so closing a
refused opener does not checkpoint committed frames into the main file.
Accepted backend activation restores ordinary close-time checkpointing. This
applies to other physical upgrades as well as `_read_sessions`. It describes
the refused operation's effects; it cannot freeze writes or checkpoints
performed by concurrent accepted owners.

[free-pages]: https://www.sqlite.org/atomiccommit.html#_do_not_journal_freelist_pages

**Why permit physical residue:** keeping every dirty page until the final
decision would require memory proportional to an arbitrarily large migration.
Late constraints, SQLite errors and native recovery can still refuse after
writes; moving a few known checks earlier cannot eliminate all such failures.
Staging a second database would add disk/copy cost and a separate publication
protocol around live SQLite owners. Neither cost is justified to preserve
unallocated page contents or invisible uncommitted frames. Retain the existing
cache/spill policy (LLP 3000.002, implementation follow-up). Linux refusal tests
compare complete schema/table/row dumps, `integrity_check` and freelist count.
The spill regression uses 1,500 legacy receipt rows with 8 KiB payloads and
4 MiB of committed free space in WAL and DELETE modes. It additionally verifies
that any changed main-file page was already a free leaf, retains the committed
WAL prefix, and proves a later accepting open can migrate. Small no-spill tests
retain their stricter whole-WAL comparisons; the checkpoint-on-close fence
still compares main-file bytes. The Darwin fence's fixture-specific byte
comparisons remain stronger witnesses, not a whole-file migration contract.

SQLite creates `_read_sessions` and its family index lazily in the transaction
that first mints a read session. A missing table means no read sessions:
authority lookups return no read credential, cleanup/logout skip the absent
table, and full-session authority remains available. Reads never create the
table; malformed storage and other SQL errors still fail closed. The
mint-binding `scope` column is installed inside bound minting's transaction.
Existing stores defer their other physical upgrades until successful backend
activation (or validated server attachment); native device side tables join
that installation transaction. Native opening also completes capture recovery,
send-revision validation, cache validation/cleanup and final metadata reads
inside the installation transaction; none may refuse after its commit.
Server `Engine::open`, `new` and `new_release` likewise read the store identity
and release metadata inside the accepting transaction, after upgrades and
metadata writes, and carry validated construction values out. Missing identities
are generated and read there; malformed identities or releases roll back the
upgrades and release pointer together. In-place server adoption checks its
fallible ephemeral-hub lock before activation, then releases it for the entire
SQLite transaction. Readers take SQLite before the hub; holding the hub while
DELETE-mode commit or spill waits for those readers would invert that order.
After commit, adoption rebinds with the validated identity. If a concurrent
reader poisoned the hub after the initial check, rebind replaces all ephemeral
state and clears poison rather than returning a refusal after durable commit.
Existing poison still refuses before activation. WAL readers holding an old
snapshot fail closed against the rebound generation.

SQLite rechecks the stored version after acquiring the writer lock for either
attachment or activation. Fast attachment keeps decoded schema/programs,
selected release, catalog and upgrade markers in that one transaction through
completion. If attachment needs semantic repair, it rechecks the exact captured
generation/schema/program bytes under activation's writer lock before physical
upgrades; activation rereads its catalog, checks the selected release and
repeats key, timeline and derived-state admission under the same lock. A
low-level handle's earlier supported version or backend is not authority after
a wait. Concurrent-opener regressions exercise version changes through all
### 1.4 The SDK

`createClient({ …, readOnly: true })` and online `createApp({ …, readOnly: true })`:

- **Verify scope with the server, before any other request.**
  - Construction validates options synchronously. It refuses `device`
    (`E_READ_ONLY_DEVICE`) and personas (`E_READ_ONLY_CREDENTIAL`).
  - The client then calls an authenticated, side-effect-free
    `/probe?scope=1`, which reports the bearer's scope. It sends nothing
    else until that answer says `read`. A full credential is refused
    (`E_READ_ONLY_CREDENTIAL`), as is a server whose probe lacks the
    capability (`E_READ_ONLY_UNSUPPORTED`).
  - The check reruns after every restore and store change, and is
    bound to the credential revision and store identity. The client never
    trusts a `scope` field in a `Session` object, which is caller-controlled
    data.
  - **`Session.scope` is optional.** Existing persisted sessions lack it,
    and `restoreSession` today deletes anything `isSession` rejects. A
    session without `scope` is a **legacy, unverified** session: it is
    preserved, never discarded or revoked for its shape. The read-only path
    probes regardless.
  - **Device admission checks the token, not the field.** Every device path
    refuses a token that starts with `ro.` (`E_READ_ONLY_DEVICE`), whatever
    its `scope` field says, before refresh, persistence or opening. That
    covers `createApp` with a device, `createLocalClient`, and restoring
    into a device app, including offline startup. This needs no probe, so
    offline device restoration of legacy full sessions keeps working
    unchanged.
  - **Nothing is exported before verification.** Until the probe has
    verified the exact current credential, `assetSource` returns no usable
    URI or headers, and it withdraws them during revalidation and after a
    failure. `assetSource` hands a bearer to a platform image loader outside
    the SDK's fetch gate, so a full token mistakenly passed to
    `readOnly: true` must not reach a loader while the probe is pending.
  - **Nothing before the probe.** Until the probe answers `read` for the
    exact current credential, `query`, `observe`, `asset` and `fetchAsset` send nothing.
    Explicit refresh refuses locally without waiting for the probe. The client builds no delivery queue, makes no
    startup refresh rotation, opens no `/changes` and accepts no manifest.
    A full token mistakenly passed to `readOnly: true` inside its refresh
    window is therefore never rotated or used, and its owner can still write
    with it.
  - The guarantee comes from the server: an auditor reads the probe result
    and the server guard, not the SDK.
- **Isolated custody.** Every key the SDK derives from `sessionKey(url)`
  has a read-only namespace, `sessionKey(url) + ".read"`, with `.epoch` and
  `.revocations` beneath it. The suffix stays inside the Expo SecureStore
  key alphabet (alphanumerics, `.`, `-`, `_`) that `storeKey` already
  guarantees. A read-only client has no write
  journal and never reads the interactive journal. Read sessions never
  enter the interactive session's slot. The read-only
  app uses only its `.read.revocations` queue for retirement, draining and
  opening-error cleanup, with scope checks before revocation. A failed
  read-only startup can never sign the interactive user out.
  **Shared app custody (Round 2).** Read-only apps use the same app state
  machine as full sessions: shared logout epochs, provisional openers, atomic
  replacement, conditional retirement, facade ownership and notifications after
  auth publication. The policy selects the `.read` key, read-scoped sign-in,
  exact-credential verification and fixed-lifetime client; it omits device and write
  owners and refuses global logout. Retirement probes each read-namespace
  credential before revoking it. **Round 4 lead ruling, superseding Round 3:**
  explicit logout means this user is signed out of this shared storage. In both
  modes it compares the last owned principal under the storage lock, after any
  already-admitted commit finishes. It tombstones and retires every credential
  of that principal in this custody, whatever its epoch: peer rotations, pending
  successors and a newer deliberate login by the same user. The tombstone fences
  same-principal logins still in flight. It never deletes or revokes a different
  principal's later login: a stale Bob facade cannot sign Alice out. If Alice is
  already stored, logout keeps her token and epoch while recording Bob's sign-out
  watermark in that same record (`snapback4SignOuts`). Sign-in snapshots these
  watermarks before dispatch and rechecks its returned principal before commit;
  same-epoch rotations carry them forward. An epoch advance discards them,
  since it already fences all openers that captured the old epoch. Thus Bob's already-pending
  login cannot replace Alice after Bob logs out; a new sign-in begun afterward
  remains allowed. Full-session
  same-user behavior on `origin/main` is preserved; the only logout behavior
### 15.4 Supplied client sessions

DX6 amends the online legacy-bearer case in [LLP 3000.000.000.001 §11](./3000.000.000.001-staging-with-real-users.rfc.md#11-wave-22-loop-repairs-dx6); complete/local validation remains as below.

Online and local constructors validate a supplied session before any transport,
device/storage work or timer. Require a non-array object with nonempty principal,
kind and token strings, a positive finite safe-integer expiresAt in milliseconds,
and valid optional scope/capExpiresAt fields. Legacy scope-less sessions remain
accepted. Expiry relative to a possibly skewed local clock is server authority;
shape validation must not reject a structurally valid past timestamp. Malformed
input throws E_SESSION with the offending field and a repair: pass the complete
Session returned by guest/login/signup or service minting, or use the existing
online token option for a bare bearer. Never include credentials in the error.
capExpiresAt without scope remains accepted legacy metadata; neither grants read authority.
Stored-session validation retains its existing auth restore path. Snapshot validated primitive fields so caller mutation cannot manufacture a NaN
timer later. Existing intentional full-session refresh and read/action expiry
semantics remain unchanged; shape validation grants no authority.

### 15.5 Qualification
### K0a implementation note

K0a introduces the crate-private reference `Committer`, `Member` and `Kind`
in `snapback4-core/src/engine/committer.rs`. Each member runs in place and
opens its original transaction through the committer. Its body selects the
original commit point; explicit rollback and implicit drop remain distinct.
Post-commit continuations run synchronously in submission order. A
`Committed<T>` acknowledgement carries hooks across the adapter/engine
boundary for activation and reset, including ephemeral rebind and expiry
continuation clearing. There is no queue, batching, retry or persisted revision.

The sequenced producers in §2, including trusted seed deletion, now enter
that seam. Activation rebuild and Following launch retain their shared
activation transaction; `seed_sequences` remains a savepoint in seed's
member. The private time-clock pin also enters as a direct member. Mutation's
publication prelude stays in place; the evaluation Postgres `mutate_once`
path is unchanged. Host wakeups remain at their existing caller boundaries.

`crates/snapback4/tests/committer_ratchet.rs` shares the store-port ratchet's
lexer and permits only falling production opener counts outside the committer
and adapters. The base has 62 matching lines; K0a leaves 49 in 11 files.
The remaining K0b candidates cover identity/session operations, action/route
admission and lifecycle, work claims/settlement/leases/presence, action/effect
steps, asset intents/views/sweeps/receipt-only reports, job/schedule admission,
opening upgrades and development replay schedule metadata. Six of the 49
are authority snapshots conservatively counted because their handle can
write. Adapter-internal transactions are excluded, so their typed identity,
release and maintenance entry points also need K0b review. This is the first
slice of K0, not completion of the stream increment.

K0 and K1 are useful whatever S1a says. K1's read sets also feed increment
E (LLP 3000.009 §7.1).

**S1a's verdict (2026-10-20).** The predicted ceiling at 0.5 ms is 4,775
commits/s, bound by the execution stage at 32 connections (about 12 round
trips per execution). That is about 40× today's commit path, but 13.5% of
raw Postgres with 32 writers against the 25% × 2 the pre-registered rule
requires. So **K2–K5 wait**. First comes the rule's named fallback:

- reduce the round trips per execution (prefetch and batched reads on the
  execution path, which also speed the in-place path);
- or raise the connection budget.

The store is then re-sized on the Hetzner pair. K0 and K1 proceed.

**S1b's re-size (2026-10-20): the rule clears, and K2–K5 proceed.** The pair
ran over a real network (RTT p50 0.58 ms) with a separate 12-thread
database host:

- Batching same-level reads and folding setup into the first flight cut
  execution to 2 round trips: 9,544/s at C = 32.
- The full-write-set commit stage reaches 4,256/s within the SLO.
- Raw Postgres on that host reaches about 5,900/s at C = 32.

So the predicted 4,256/s clears the required 2,945/s by 45%. Two
consequences follow:

- **Round-trip reduction becomes part of K3/K4.** It also speeds in-place
  members.
- **The single committer is a structural ceiling.** Raw Postgres scales
  past it at C ≥ 64, and the ceiling is recorded in child C's ledger as a
  design-choice limit. On the 96-core host behind the proxy (S1a), the
  same rule failed, because raw Postgres there was six times faster. The
  25%-of-raw bar depends on the hardware, and the absolute per-store commit
  rate is what child C reports.

# What Snapback 4 Does Not Do

Longer than the doing-list on purpose. Moving something off it means writing one
line naming what it unblocks and taking something off the doing-list in the same
change. Nothing comes off during slice one (LLP 3000 §3: the clock is four weeks).

## The bars

The bakeoff gates in [LLP 3000 §3](../llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md) are the
bars: the loop bar, hub acquisition within 2× the measured floor, client bytes
under the competitor-anchored budgets, the six scenarios green in the simulator
on the hub fixture. Everything not required to pass them does not exist yet.

## Not building

- **A second authoring language.** Quarry, one spelling, copied from Snapback 2 (LLP 1004, LLP 1007 §4b). TypeScript is for seeds, journeys, effect bodies and the client.
- **Byte- and step-priced certification.** No closure, heap, output or step ceilings at `check`; the certificate certifies shape, uniformity, completeness class and prediction eligibility. Resources are metered at runtime (LLP 2000.000 §6).
- **A per-query result cache.** No range receipts, validators, halos, absence evidence, coverage spans, extent facts, held directories, fact integrity or swap journals. The client replicates declared partitions with one watermark each (LLP 2000.000 §7).
- **Engine-minted ids for client inserts, grants, ID slices.** Ids are client-minted, time-ordered, 128-bit; the engine mints for its own writes.
- **The `drain` protocol, follower-tab inbox rows, `defineItems`, `withHistory`, `mutationTarget`, `receipt.source`, `local.caughtUp`, `partial` reason codes.** The card (`packages/snapback4/src/contract.ts`) is the whole client vocabulary; `why` carries the rest.
- **`retain` as a receipt rule.** `until-revoked` and `delivered-history` survive as the client's leave policy on a scope tombstone.
- **JavaScript in the data plane.** Queries and mutations run in the Rust interpreter inside the SQLite transaction, and in the same interpreter on the client (host decided at week 3 by measurement, LLP 2000.000 §9). A *job* may be the app's own Rust, hosted as wasm through the interpreter's operations under the job's meter (LLP 3000.005, the Moves line below); a query or mutation may not.
- **An embedded JS engine for effects.** The data plane never calls out. `emit` writes the effect durably in the mutation's commit and an external worker holding a service token claims, performs and settles it over the wire (`snapback4/worker`); the four reference effects run on the builder's Node (LLP 2000.000 §10, decided 2026-09-13).
- **Committed generated code; an auth mode; a supervised bundler.** As Snapback 2 (`rules/NOT-DOING.md`): types by inference, personas not modes, `SNAPBACK_URL` is the client contract.
- **A TypeScript twin of the interpreter.** Retired by the author's ruling of 2026-09-13 (LLP 3000 §7): one implementation, in Rust, on every host — the core's SQLite under the native module, memory under wasm on the web with the host persisting each commit's changes. The web pays 312,924 B gzip before the diet where the twin's tier paid 16,842; the conformance obligation between two interpreters ends. Expo Go runs the online tier.
- **More than one hosted target, an admin console, media transcoding or thumbnails, push delivery itself (a worker's), cron syntax beyond `every N at HH:MM`, vector or relevance-ranked search (word-prefix search is in: `words(..)` and `matching`), federation, multi-region, device-to-device sync, at-rest encryption claims.** After the clock, on evidence.
- **General observability beyond `why`, `trace`, `logs`, `data`, `sql`, server operation latency, and bounded client subscription inspection.** The `sql` verb is read-only inspection of the developer's own store (LLP 3000.007 §8.8), never an application surface. Separately declared application SQL reads are admitted for increment D (LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4; the 2026-09-16 Moves line below), not implemented by this inspection verb.
- **A guide corpus.** The compiler's facts, one language card and keyword cards, as Snapback 2; no freshness dates. Every card's Quarry is compiled by the suite and the language card is run, so a card cannot promise a form the parser refuses.

- **Partial union delivery, two group audiences, a public branch.** A union syncs principal-column branches and at most one group branch, each a whole disjunct of the read rule (LLP 3000.036.000.000); a branch with an extra condition, a second group or a `public` branch is refused with the rewrite, never partially delivered; service grants add no device audience (LLP 3000.007 §20.6). A union does not combine with delivered history, read-through children, synced or row-keyed views, owner keys, timelines, private field owners, acquisition windows or direct/inherited expiry on content or membership scopes.
- **Exact horizon maintenance.** The horizon bounds acquisition (a snapshot, a bootstrap); the log never evicts or back-fills to hold a device's group at exactly N rows. A local read past the acquired horizon by key says `complete: false`.

- **Clock expressions in rules.** A read rule compares with `=` and `!=` only; `.expiresAt > now` remains a mutation `require`. Stored lifetimes use `not before .col` for server-controlled opening and `expire at .col` for expiry. The engine publishes opening commits; devices receive no unopened payload. The supported compositions and explicit exclusions are in LLP 3000.036.003.
- **A jobs framework.** No priorities, worker pools or dead letters: one FIFO of runs, one chunk at a time, a refused chunk parked with its refusal. Actions, effects and scheduled actions carry per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation, and a record per external step (LLP 3000.009 §5.2; the Moves line below). Missed period boundaries while a server is down fire once on return, not once per missed period.
- **Asset processing.** No transcoding, thumbnails, rungs or HEIC: the bytes are served as uploaded; JPEG, PNG, WebP and MP4 only; image dimensions and optional MP4 duration read from the header; MP4 dimensions are not extracted.
- **Search beyond word prefixes.** One tokenizer (NFC, lowercase, alphanumeric words), one index shape (`scope, words(cols), order.., id`), one read (`matching`): every term a prefix of some word, the first term's words merged as lanes, bounded by the meter in a uniform scope, at most 32 words in an admitted non-uniform scope. No stemming, no ranking by relevance, no phrase or mid-word matching, no per-language folding, no intersection index for the second term (it filters the page).
- **An identity provider.** Guests, password accounts, service seats, and sessions a service mints for accounts the application authenticated itself. No phone, OAuth, magic links or MFA in the engine: an application's worker verifies those and mints the session.
- **No multi-server presence, no presence history or last-seen storage, no broadcast event stream, no connection-bound rows.** `connected[p]` stays server-wide, counting held polls and their 35 s grace; a live query that may read it re-runs in the background about every 5 seconds to stay current (LLP 3000.018). Typing and viewing use short-lived, authorized ephemeral tables in one server process (LLP 3000.017); they never sync or decide durable invariants. A device does not know presence and says so.

## Process not doing

- **2026-09-28 — ordinary mixed search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3, lane OS round 1).
  Admit the SDK's bounded merge of current server rows and certified Left images
  for the proved single time-ordered word page. This removes application-owned
  online/held selection for that shape. One server call per requested page;
  recomputation pays again from consumed positions. The coordinator shares the
  existing device read allowance. No durable search session, extra synced rows,
  new authority or additional allowance is admitted.

- **2026-09-28 — inferred current-scope word search** (LLP 3000.028 §3.3,
  lane OS). A single bare ordinary word page may derive the exact non-expiring
  membership audience for the existing compact server plan. This removes the
  requirement to repeat that audience predicate in this narrow server query.
  The compiler supplies a scoped words index when absent; it is an ordinary
  schema index, maintained on writes and on devices, with no extra synced rows
  or new authority. The device keeps its original bare scan. Round 0 left ordered composition
  and combined accounting outside its independent subset; round 1 above admits
  that bounded SDK composition. Durable search sessions remain excluded.
- **2026-10-12 — union sync audiences** (LLP 3000.036.000.000, workstream UNION, two blind review rounds). A table syncs to the union of principal-column audiences and one group audience; a device holds a row while any branch admits its viewer. What it unblocks: owner-or-team, buyer-or-seller and owner-or-staff tables offline for every reader, the shape three app farms asked for. Taken off: "one `sync` audience per table". Partial delivery, two groups and a public branch stay excluded (above).
- **2026-09-28 — explicit owner-key coverage** (LLP 3000.028 §3.4, Lane OK commission). A public window may opt into a compiler-owned, horizon-free principal key partition to answer ordinary own-key existence offline. Taken off: the exclusion of narrower platform key distribution beside one public window. General union audiences, full own-row replication and per-query absence caches remain excluded. The projection uses existing metered maintains, adoption and whole-partition publication; existing windows do not expand.
- **2026-09-26 — a live query keeps presence current** (LLP 3000.018). A 0.4.2 probe found an observed `connected[p]` reporting `fresh: true` while stale for over a minute, and a comparative builder polled around it. What it unblocks: online marks and presence-aware lists that stay current. Taken off: "a presence stream" as an exclusion for live queries; a presence API, last-seen storage and multi-server presence stay out. Under the author's standing authorization for reworks that make queries excellent (2026-09-25).

- **No drift registries, inventories, line-pinned diagnostics registries, claim digests or frozen-record gates.** Generated projections are built, not committed.
- **No gate bound to an exact commit; no push gated on a battery.** Five blocking checks in sixty seconds, run before push; everything slower on a schedule with bounded tickets.
- **No ceiling raised in slice one.** Budgets are anchored to a competitor's measured number and recorded once in the journal.
- **No standing panel** except the two blind reviews LLP 3000 decision 4 names (partition inversion; the meter's cut on non-uniform scans). The author explicitly commissioned the one-off LLP 3000.008 panel and disjoint Astra implementation lanes on 2026-09-14; its bounded disposition is recorded in that umbrella and LLP 3000 §7.
- **No new LLP number** without first extending LLP 3000 or LLP 2000.000; no spec without running code (`rules/RULES.md`: only implemented specs bind).
- **No second session on the core** during slice one; lanes take the periphery.
- **No test matrix as the primary harness.** The simulator is; real-browser and device witnesses cover only what it cannot reach.
- **No files over 1,500 lines**, including ones copied from Snapback 2. Split on
  the way in. The one exception is the package's published documents
  (`packages/snapback4/README.md` and its linked references): the rule exists to
  keep a *source* file reviewable, and the 0.2.25 calibration measured the cost of
  applying it to a document — splitting the README was that run's single largest
  scoring loss, because the builder then had four places to look instead of one
  (LLP 3000.007 §20.19). A published document is bounded by whether a builder can
  find one way in, not by a line count; the census that enforces this rule covers
  source, and says so.

## Deliberately worse

- No compatibility with Snapback 1 or Snapback 2 apps, packages or on-disk formats.
- No API stability before the clock; every publish is `0.0.x`.
- Sparse prose documentation; the diagnostics and the recipes carry the load.
- One hosted target, one platform binary at a time. SQLite remains the default store; Postgres as a production store is being built under LLP 3000.009.001 (Moves, 2026-10-20) and is not yet a supported deployment target.

## Moves

- **2026-10-20 — Snapback 4 on Postgres moves onto the doing-list** (author: "commit and push these things, then do your \"what i'd do next\" things, then get this working on postgres and measure it"; LLP 3000.009.001 and its children). Unblocks: the store-port extraction on SQLite (P0, including the `Committer` seam with the serial batch); the Postgres adapters for every durable-state family and `serve`/`deploy`/`doctor` over Postgres (P1), with store-held fencing, the declared HA mode and `BlobStore`; the optimistic committer, commissioned on 2026-10-19 and built after its execution stage is sized; the scale program of LLP 3000.009.001.002 with its raw-Postgres, Supabase and Convex controls on fleet machines; and a research note comparing replicated SQLite with Postgres for applications that outgrow one SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation-only framing of the Postgres adapter. Stands: no publication or managed-service deployment authority, no lowering of Quarry to SQL, no SQL writes to managed data, one core owner. Several serving processes (P2), log retention, live graduation (LLP 3000.009.001.001) and the async-safe external anchor each wait for their own Moves line.

- **2026-10-13 — stored lower lifetimes (`not before`) are commissioned by PAGE2** (LLP 3000.036.003). This unblocks timed disclosure without an application publication job. Per-application jobs that flip a publication flag leave this admitted path; clock expressions in rules, captured lower lifetimes for read-through children, and boundary recounts for maintained data remain excluded. HS owns handshake transport; PAGE2 declares the new semantic capability.

- **2026-10-06 — exact Following history (FH delegated round-1 ruling).**
  Admit a Following-specific server traversal and bounded volatile frontier/replay
  state under LLP 3000.026 §9. The approved design reads exact history online
  from the first page; the SDK will own continuation. The first milestone is internal event metadata
  and the oracle, with no public completeness claim. Taken off: a blanket ban on
  state for this one expiring read. General result caches, resumable queries,
  device traversal, inbox coverage certificates and history-only blocks remain
  excluded. Existing inbox bytes and runtime ceilings remain unchanged.

- **2026-09-26 — server operation latency (LLP 3000.019 §3.7).** Calibration #22
  measured each read once and could not claim repeated p95/degradation testing.
  Unblocks percentile measurements of the application's own load without a
  custom timing harness. Taken off: a latency harness written by the builder;
  general metrics, tracing infrastructure and an admin console remain excluded.

- **2026-09-23 — Quarry states intent moves onto the doing-list** (Charlie:
  "if you need to try making *significant* architectural reworks in order to
  make the data modeling, queries, and scalability be excellent and elegant,
  you can experiment with that"; LLP 3000.013). Unblocks reads that state
  predicates and order, one `view` declaration for derived data, Following
  without caps, jobs and aggregates without author-chosen bounds, an app
  object and an offline web shell. Taken off: index names and author-chosen
  engine numbers on the paved path; the timeline's promotion, pull routes and
  caps; the "Time in rules" refusal is unchanged. Stands: one interpreter, no
  JavaScript in the data plane, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no relevance-ranked
  search.

- **2026-09-16 — the remaining general-purpose program moves onto the doing-list**
  (Charlie: "complete everything ... we'll remove services when we are done
  with the workroom test rather than wait to add them in ... use the fleet";
  LLP 3000.009 §14.7). Unblocks the §7 integrated-service baseline, C3 production
  Postgres, D's authorized database capabilities and measurement-led C2/E
  work while Workroom continues. Taken off: waiting for Workroom before
  starting services, and keeping production Postgres integration outside the
  current implementation program. The completed Workroom record drives a
  retain/remove pass. Stands: one interpreter and managed writer, no managed
  SQL writes, no JavaScript transactions, no SQL in the first Workroom cell,
  no invented performance/real-provider evidence, no expo-build-1000 load,
  and no new publication or managed-service deployment authority.

- **2026-09-16 — application SQL reads move onto the doing-list** (Charlie: "admit application SQL reads"; LLP 3000.009 §6.2, §14.4, increment D). Unblocks reporting and search over authorized projection inputs and declared database-owned tables, with authorization before selection, ordering and limits. Taken off in the same change: the inspection-only exclusion for application SQL and D's pending-author gate. Implementation and qualification remain pending; SQLite and Postgres halves ship together, channels follow candidates, and A1–A3 plus the fixture pack stay first. Stands: no SQL writes into managed data, no lowering Quarry to SQL, no SQL in the first Snapback brief cell, and no publication authorization.

- **2026-09-15 — a Postgres evaluation backend moves onto the doing-list** (author: "Build the evaluation prototype"). Unblocks concrete evaluation of storage agnosticism, interpreter/database round trips and transactional sync ordering. This bounded prototype replaces full CLI/deployment integration as the next backend task; those product surfaces remain off this increment's doing-list. It shares Quarry, rules, maintains and partition logic, but uses an experimental Postgres store. Native SQL exists only as an explicitly separate comparison workload, not an application escape hatch. SQLite remains the production and local-device store. See [the experiment](./experiments/postgres/README.md).

- **2026-09-15 — the server half of LLP 3000.009 moves onto the doing-list** (author: "begin implementation using astra xhigh to code"; LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increments A1–A3). Unblocks the online-only brief's invitation, webhook and scheduled journeys without a second application server: work records whose authority is bound at admission, ids bound to their operation and arguments, declared action results, a generated handler context with a durable record per external step, declared routes and webhooks, scheduled actions under service seats, and `serve` supervising the runtime. Taken off in the same change: "A jobs framework" narrows — actions, effects and scheduled actions get per-operation retries, deadlines and cancellation; priorities, worker pools and dead letters stay out — and the actions card's "supervise the worker as an ordinary Node process" leaves. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no identity provider in the engine, and no `ctx.sql`, projections or SQL reads in this increment.

- **2026-09-15 — the Postgres access boundary moves onto the doing-list** (LLP 3000.009 §10.2, increment C1). Unblocks measuring Postgres behind a batched access boundary instead of row-at-a-time calls: transaction-scoped prefetch, a pipelined commit tail, one portable index-key bound checked on both stores, and the corpus, conformance and device suites on both stores. Taken off in the same change: the evaluation prototype's single-adapter framing, in which the only next step was an unbatched store. Stands: Postgres is not a hosted or deployable target; its refusals of assets, jobs, actions and schedules, and `serve`, `deploy` and backup on Postgres, wait for increment C3.

- **2026-09-13 — word search, effects, service seats and presence move onto the doing-list** (author: "add all the things we need for Clique and Interview"; the full-brief Clique record's remaining capability lines and Interview's adapter responsibilities). What it unblocks: Clique M5's three search scopes online and offline on one index, M6's push seam without connection leases hand-built in tables, M8's digest emitted rather than logged into a table, Interview's phone sign-in and model workers as a service that mints sessions instead of proxying every request, and the ranked feed of LLP 3000.004 on the `feeds` card. Taken off in the same change: "an embedded JS engine for effects, until measured" (the engine never calls out; a worker does), "word search" from the package's exclusions. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, no relevance ranking, no identity provider in the engine.
- **2026-09-12 — expiry, assets and jobs move onto the doing-list** (author: "complete snapback4 to the point where the entire clique benchmark can be run"). What it unblocks: the struck half of the Clique brief (M4 media, M5 view-once and expiry with no client open, M8's scheduled digest). Taken off in the same change: the "one photo path" and "cron syntax" exclusions above narrow to transcoding and to cron beyond `every N [at HH:MM]`; the "time in rules" exclusion stands, answered by `expire at .col` rather than a rule.
- **2026-09-13 — native jobs on (author: "write up an LLP about this and then implement").** Unblocks the scoring half of a feed — floats, vectors, real loops, a typed schema — without putting any of it into Quarry, and gives the model half of LLP 3000.004 a seat inside the bounds: one chunk per commit, the job ceilings, fuel, no I/O. Off the doing-list in the same change: the "external ranker with a service seat" of LLP 3000.004 §6.5 is no longer the only shape for compute over the store; it remains the shape for the network. Ranked and vector search, effects execution and an embedded JS engine stay above.
- **2026-09-13 — `sql`, a fifth named observability verb, moves onto the doing-list** (author ruling on LLP 3000.007 §14.10). Read-only SQL over generated per-table views of the developer's own store, under `dev` or against a `--release` directory; never rule-filtered, never an application surface. What it unblocks: the ground truth two Clique essays said Snapback lacked beside Postgres ("there is no underlying Postgres to query"), and the inspection the 0.2.6 Astra lane did by copying its SQLite file. Taken off in the same change: SQL reads as an application query surface (LLP 3000.007 §9.1) leave the doing-list until the reporting gap is measured after the language relaxations; reporting is Quarry's, inspection is `sql`'s. Stands: no JavaScript in the data plane, one authoring language.

- **2026-09-14 — the simulator witness is published for app builders (LLP 3000.007 §20.10, S30).** Unblocks native offline proof artifacts; replaces the repository-only Metro witness on the doing-list. Real-browser and device witnesses still cover only what the deterministic simulator cannot reach.

- **2026-09-14 — development clock control (S27, calibration F5)** moves onto the doing-list to unblock expiry and scheduled-digest tests without real-minute waits or backdated seeds; waiting on real time leaves the development test path, while production clock controls stay off the doing-list.

- **2026-09-25 — ephemeral tables enact the standing realtime line of LLP 3000.009 §12 (LLP 3000.017).** Unblocks typing and viewing without durable outbox traffic. Nothing leaves the doing-list: this enacts an already admitted scope. Last-seen storage, multi-server presence, a separate presence stream and ephemeral authority over durable invariants remain excluded.

- **2026-09-30 — bounded client subscription inspection (author's PR round-0 task)**
  moves onto the doing-list: query-group queue/in-flight/withdrawal counts and
  bounded latency/cause samples explain mounted screens flashing empty. Taken off
  the doing-list: builders must reconstruct this pressure from ad-hoc console
  instrumentation. General observability beyond the named verbs remains excluded;
  there is no telemetry transport or server admin console (LLP 3000.023 §10).

+// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#11-wave-22-loop-repairs-dx6 — hidden descendants cannot manufacture rendered evidence.
+import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
+import type { Locator, Selectors } from "playwright-core";
+
+export const renderedSelector = `sb4rendered${randomUUID().replaceAll("-", "")}`;
+const registrations = new WeakMap<Selectors, Promise<void>>();
+export async function registerRenderedSelector(selectors: Selectors): Promise<void> {
+  let registration = registrations.get(selectors);
+  if (!registration) {
+    registration = selectors.register(renderedSelector, renderedTextEngine, { contentScript: true });
+    registrations.set(selectors, registration);
+  }
+  await registration;
+}
+
+// Playwright serializes this function into its isolated selector world. Keep it
+// self-contained: no application script, global callback or injected DOM marker.
+function renderedTextEngine() {
+  let observedText = "";
+  function queryAll(root: Document | Element | ShadowRoot, selector: string): Element[] {
+    const options = JSON.parse(selector) as { source: string; flags: string; regex: boolean; exact: boolean };
+    const regex = options.regex ? new RegExp(options.source, options.flags) : null;
+    const needle = options.source.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
+    const found: Element[] = [];
+    const matches = (text: string) => {
+      if (!text) return false;
+      if (regex) { regex.lastIndex = 0; return regex.test(text); }
+      return options.exact ? text === needle : text.toLowerCase().includes(needle.toLowerCase());
+    };
+    const walk = (node: Node, ancestorSuppressed = false): { text: string; matched: boolean } => {
+      if (node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE) return { text: ancestorSuppressed ? "" : node.textContent ?? "", matched: false };
+      if (node.nodeType !== Node.ELEMENT_NODE && node.nodeType !== Node.DOCUMENT_NODE && node.nodeType !== Node.DOCUMENT_FRAGMENT_NODE) return { text: "", matched: false };
+      const element = node.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE ? node as HTMLElement : null;
+      if (element && (/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SCRIPT|STYLE|NOSCRIPT|TEMPLATE)$/.test(element.tagName) || element.isContentEditable)) return { text: "", matched: false };
+      const style = element ? getComputedStyle(element) : null;
+      if (style?.display === "none" || style?.opacity === "0") return { text: "", matched: false };
+      const suppressed = style ? style.visibility === "hidden" || style.visibility === "collapse" : ancestorSuppressed;
+      const nodes = element?.shadowRoot ? element.shadowRoot.childNodes
+        : element?.tagName === "SLOT" ? ((element as HTMLSlotElement).assignedNodes({flatten:true}).length ? (element as HTMLSlotElement).assignedNodes({flatten:true}) : element.childNodes) : node.childNodes;
+      let text = "", descendantMatch = false;
+      for (const child of nodes) {
+        const result = walk(child, suppressed);
+        // Block boundaries are whitespace, never concatenated words from
+        // unrelated paragraph siblings. Inline RN spans retain their text.
+        const block = child.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE && /^(block|flex|grid|table|list-item)/.test(getComputedStyle(child as Element).display);
+        text += block ? ` ${result.text} ` : result.text;
+        descendantMatch ||= result.matched;
+      }
+      const normalized = text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
+      const box = element?.getBoundingClientRect();
+      const visible = element && !suppressed && box && box.width > 0 && box.height > 0;
+      const ownMatch = !!visible && !/^(HTML|BODY)$/.test(element.tagName) && matches(normalized);
+      if (ownMatch && !descendantMatch) found.push(element);
+      return { text, matched: ownMatch || descendantMatch };
+    };
+    observedText = walk(root).text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
+    return found;
+  }
+  return { queryAll, query: (root: Document | Element | ShadowRoot, selector: string) => queryAll(root, selector)[0] ?? null,
+    text: (root: Element) => { queryAll(root, '{"source":"","flags":"","regex":false,"exact":true}'); return observedText; } };
+}
+
+export function visibleText(locator: Locator, timeout: number): Promise<string> {
+  // Build the function in Node, then let Playwright serialize it. Passing an
+  // arrow as a string would evaluate to the function rather than invoke it.
+  const reader = new Function("element", `return (${renderedTextEngine.toString()})().text(element);`) as (element: Element) => string;
+  return locator.evaluate(reader, undefined, { timeout });
+}
+
+// Optional peer: CLI/process helpers still import and run when it is absent.
+// Await registration before exposing the synchronous locator constructor.
+try { await registerRenderedSelector((await import("playwright-core")).selectors); }
+catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND" && (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "MODULE_NOT_FOUND") throw error; }

+/** @internal Shared by the Node proof kit and the native browser driver. */
+export function webWitnessObserverScript(): string {
+  return `(() => {
+    const register = (${registerWebWitness.toString()});
+    let selected;
+    Object.defineProperty(window, Symbol.for("snapback4.proof.app"), { configurable: false, writable: false, value: app => {
+      if (selected) return;
+      selected = app;
+      register(app);
+      const bridge = window.__snapback4Witness;
+      Object.freeze(bridge);
+      Object.defineProperty(window, "__snapback4Witness", { configurable: false, writable: false, value: bridge });
+    }});
+  })();`;

 /** Run an event handler with an explicit write claim that survives awaited uploads.
  * @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000.000#11 — only explicit per-arm custody survives an async handler.
  * Call synchronously from the clicked control. Use the supplied mutate inside
// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#11-wave-22-loop-repairs-dx6 — hidden descendants cannot manufacture rendered evidence.
import { randomUUID } from "node:crypto";
import type { Locator, Selectors } from "playwright-core";

export const renderedSelector = `sb4rendered${randomUUID().replaceAll("-", "")}`;
const registrations = new WeakMap<Selectors, Promise<void>>();
export async function registerRenderedSelector(selectors: Selectors): Promise<void> {
  let registration = registrations.get(selectors);
  if (!registration) {
    registration = selectors.register(renderedSelector, renderedTextEngine, { contentScript: true });
    registrations.set(selectors, registration);
  }
  await registration;
}

// Playwright serializes this function into its isolated selector world. Keep it
// self-contained: no application script, global callback or injected DOM marker.
function renderedTextEngine() {
  let observedText = "";
  function queryAll(root: Document | Element | ShadowRoot, selector: string): Element[] {
    const options = JSON.parse(selector) as { source: string; flags: string; regex: boolean; exact: boolean };
    const regex = options.regex ? new RegExp(options.source, options.flags) : null;
    const needle = options.source.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    const found: Element[] = [];
    const matches = (text: string) => {
      if (!text) return false;
      if (regex) { regex.lastIndex = 0; return regex.test(text); }
      return options.exact ? text === needle : text.toLowerCase().includes(needle.toLowerCase());
    };
    const walk = (node: Node, ancestorSuppressed = false): { text: string; matched: boolean } => {
      if (node.nodeType === Node.TEXT_NODE) return { text: ancestorSuppressed ? "" : node.textContent ?? "", matched: false };
      if (node.nodeType !== Node.ELEMENT_NODE && node.nodeType !== Node.DOCUMENT_NODE && node.nodeType !== Node.DOCUMENT_FRAGMENT_NODE) return { text: "", matched: false };
      const element = node.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE ? node as HTMLElement : null;
      if (element && (/^(INPUT|TEXTAREA|SCRIPT|STYLE|NOSCRIPT|TEMPLATE)$/.test(element.tagName) || element.isContentEditable)) return { text: "", matched: false };
      const style = element ? getComputedStyle(element) : null;
      if (style?.display === "none" || style?.opacity === "0") return { text: "", matched: false };
      const suppressed = style ? style.visibility === "hidden" || style.visibility === "collapse" : ancestorSuppressed;
      const nodes = element?.shadowRoot ? element.shadowRoot.childNodes
        : element?.tagName === "SLOT" ? ((element as HTMLSlotElement).assignedNodes({flatten:true}).length ? (element as HTMLSlotElement).assignedNodes({flatten:true}) : element.childNodes) : node.childNodes;
      let text = "", descendantMatch = false;
      for (const child of nodes) {
        const result = walk(child, suppressed);
        // Block boundaries are whitespace, never concatenated words from
        // unrelated paragraph siblings. Inline RN spans retain their text.
        const block = child.nodeType === Node.ELEMENT_NODE && /^(block|flex|grid|table|list-item)/.test(getComputedStyle(child as Element).display);
        text += block ? ` ${result.text} ` : result.text;
        descendantMatch ||= result.matched;
      }
      const normalized = text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
      const box = element?.getBoundingClientRect();
      const visible = element && !suppressed && box && box.width > 0 && box.height > 0;
      const ownMatch = !!visible && !/^(HTML|BODY)$/.test(element.tagName) && matches(normalized);
      if (ownMatch && !descendantMatch) found.push(element);
      return { text, matched: ownMatch || descendantMatch };
    };
    observedText = walk(root).text.replace(/\s+/g, " ").trim();
    return found;
  }
  return { queryAll, query: (root: Document | Element | ShadowRoot, selector: string) => queryAll(root, selector)[0] ?? null,
    text: (root: Element) => { queryAll(root, '{"source":"","flags":"","regex":false,"exact":true}'); return observedText; } };
}

export function visibleText(locator: Locator, timeout: number): Promise<string> {
  // Build the function in Node, then let Playwright serialize it. Passing an
  // arrow as a string would evaluate to the function rather than invoke it.
  const reader = new Function("element", `return (${renderedTextEngine.toString()})().text(element);`) as (element: Element) => string;
  return locator.evaluate(reader, undefined, { timeout });
}

// Optional peer: CLI/process helpers still import and run when it is absent.
// Await registration before exposing the synchronous locator constructor.
try { await registerRenderedSelector((await import("playwright-core")).selectors); }
catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND" && (error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code !== "MODULE_NOT_FOUND") throw error; }

# DX6 frontend and proof text evidence

Commits: `babf5cf562` (ReadState starters, historical template custody, proof-owned bridge), `129911f3a5` (test scratch cleanup), `37aa4103ed` (locked public observer and visible-text engine).

Both todo starter lists use the SDK ReadState and distinguish proven absence from partial empty answers. Partial/stale rows and pagination survive. Password starter client modules, including production recipes, omit web witness registration and witness-persona selection. The docs-local component is ReadNotice. Exact previous 0.4.37 templates remain recognized for adaptation; custom source remains application-owned.

The public same-origin proof observer is immutable, selects the first App on each page and locks its bridge. Same-App explicit generated-API registration enriches it; another explicit App refuses E_WITNESS_APP_SELECTION. This is a test observer, not an authentication or confidentiality boundary. Normal launches publish no App global. Separate App instances use separate pages.

The custom Playwright selector traverses composed visible text, including open shadow roots and wrapping labels, excludes hidden/CSS-hidden/zero-opacity/editor text and keeps independent matches ambiguous. Strings remain exact and case-sensitive by default; explicit substring is case-insensitive; regex preserves its source and flags against normalized visible text. waitForFresh compares and returns that same normalized visible text, so a hidden descendant change cannot produce freshness, including after locator chaining. The module registers its optional installed peer before exposing the synchronous constructor; withBrowser also registers the resolved caller peer. A completely foreign Playwright registry must use the same installed peer or withBrowser; it fails loudly rather than falling back.

+fn untouched_wave22_lists_and_clients_adapt_without_rewriting_owned_source() {
+    for platform in [Platform::Web, Platform::Expo] {
+        for password in [false, true] {
+            let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(format!("sb4-w22-adapt-{}-{platform:?}-{password}", std::process::id()));
+            std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("snapback")).unwrap();
+            std::fs::create_dir_all(dir.join("src")).unwrap();
+            let schema = include_str!("starter/schema.q");
+            let schema = if password { schema.replace("use identity ", "use identity password ") } else { schema.to_owned() };
+            std::fs::write(dir.join("snapback/schema.q"), schema).unwrap();
+            let (app, client) = starter::wave22_screens(platform, None, &Body::Todos, password);
+            let (app_path, client_path) = platform.paths();
+            std::fs::write(dir.join(app_path), app).unwrap();
+            std::fs::write(dir.join(client_path), &client).unwrap();
+            let (changes, _) = leftovers(&dir);
+            assert!(changes.iter().any(|change| change.path == app_path), "{platform:?} password={password}: upgrade teaches ReadState");
+            assert_eq!(changes.iter().any(|change| change.path == client_path), password, "{platform:?} password={password}: only password upgrades remove witness plumbing; guest persona and direct-SDK proofs stay supported");
+            std::fs::write(dir.join(client_path), format!("{client}\n// application-owned edit\n")).unwrap();
+            let (changes, _) = leftovers(&dir);
+            assert!(!changes.iter().any(|change| change.path == app_path || change.path == client_path));
+            std::fs::remove_dir_all(dir).unwrap();
+        }
+    }

+/** Delivery receipts are advisory and never block installation or compilation. */
+export function acknowledgeWarnings(root, version) {
+  const receipt = join(root, '.snapback4/project-warnings-version.json');
+  const temporary = `${receipt}.${randomUUID()}.tmp`;
+  try {
+    mkdirSync(dirname(receipt), {recursive:true});
+    writeFileSync(temporary, JSON.stringify({version}) + '\n', {flag:'wx'});
+    renameSync(temporary, receipt);
+  } finally { try { rmSync(temporary, {force:true}); } catch { /* Advisory only. */ } }

// @ref LLP 3000.009.001.000#32-the-rule — inspection SQL stays inside the adapter.

pub struct Inspection {
    conn: Connection,
    denied: Arc<AtomicU8>,
}
pub use crate::inspection::InspectionRows;
impl Inspection {
    pub fn open(path: &Path) -> Result<Self> {
        let conn = Connection::open_with_flags(path, OpenFlags::SQLITE_OPEN_READ_ONLY)
            .map_err(store_error)?;
        Ok(Self {
            conn,
            denied: Arc::new(AtomicU8::new(0)),
        })
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#11-wave-22-loop-repairs-dx6 — inspection refuses future formats before exposing any records.
    pub fn begin_snapshot(&self) -> Result<()> {
        self.conn.execute_batch("BEGIN DEFERRED").map_err(store_error)?;
        super::Store::check_version(&self.conn).map_err(|error| ("E_SCHEMA_CAPABILITY", error.to_string()))
    }
    pub fn build_views(&self, schema: &Schema, ephemeral: impl FnMut(&str)) -> Result<()> {
        self.build_retained_views(schema, false, false, ephemeral)
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.000.000#19.4 — active and retained records share one snapshot and authorizer.

                ephemeral(name);
