# Staging with real users

Staging runs real password users and application rules under serve. Use it for shared previews and probe:serve, with roles provisioned by a local operator.

```quarry
use identity password
table roles:
  principal: principal
  role: enum('admin', 'member')
  unique principal
  immutable principal
  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:operator'
  insert <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  update <- viewer = 'service:operator'
  online only
mutation provision(principal: principal, role: enum('admin', 'member')):
  require viewer = 'service:operator' else NOT_OPERATOR
  return upsert roles[principal] { principal, role }
query myRole():
  return roles[viewer]
table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text 1..200
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  online only
mutation add(body: text 1..200):
  return insert notes { owner: viewer, body }
query myNotes(c: cursor ?):
  return notes where .owner = viewer first 20 by .id after c
```

Use `serve` for staging, shared previews and `probe:serve`. It enforces the same
password identity and rules as production. `dev` always advertises
`development:true` in `/auth/config`, even with closed doors; probes should
refuse it. `guide deploy` covers release evolution; PROD2 owns starter adaptation.

## Password roles and operator provisioning

Save this complete source as `snapback/schema.q` in an app with snapback4 installed.
Signup creates an account without a role. Only the local filesystem operator can
bootstrap this roster. The insert rule enforces the boundary independently of
the mutation. SEC owns self-promotion diagnostics and invariant proofs.

## Stand up staging

Run `npx snapback4 check` to generate the API. Save this as `staging.mjs` next to
snapback/, then run `node staging.mjs`. Use `node staging.mjs --once` for CI setup
and shutdown. The deployment and private credentials file remain for inspection;
choose fresh names for each independent run. Add both to .gitignore. PORT_LO and
PORT_HI optionally choose a port range. The printed URL belongs to `serve`.

```js
import { openSync, writeFileSync, closeSync } from "node:fs";
import { deploy, startServe } from "snapback4/proof";
import { api } from "./snapback/generated/api.ts";

await deploy("staging", "snapback", { args: ["--staging"] });
const host = await startServe("staging", { seedUsers: {
  roles: { admin: 2, member: 2 }, provision: api.provision,
} });
try {
  // This file contains real passwords and bearer tokens. Never commit it.
  const fd = openSync("staging-users.json", "wx", 0o600);
  try { writeFileSync(fd, JSON.stringify(host.sessions)); } finally { closeSync(fd); }
  for (const accounts of Object.values(host.sessions)) {
    for (const account of accounts) {
      const response = await fetch(host.url + "/m/add", {
        method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json", authorization: `Bearer ${account.token}` },
        body: JSON.stringify({ args: { body: `Welcome ${account.role}` } }),
        signal: AbortSignal.timeout(10000),
      });
      if ((await response.json()).state !== "sent") throw Error("staging data write refused");
    }
  }
  console.log(JSON.stringify({ backendUrl: host.url, credentialsFile: "staging-users.json" }));
  if (!process.argv.includes("--once")) await new Promise(resolve => {
    process.once("SIGTERM", resolve); process.once("SIGINT", resolve);
  });
} finally { await host.stop(); }
```

`host.seedAccounts({roles, provision, args?})` performs additional batches; an
optional `args(account)` merges over `{principal, role}` (explicit keys win).
Use `service: "ops"` for a provisioning mutation authorized as `service:ops`.
Each call creates new random email/password pairs and returns role arrays of
`{kind:"user",role,email,password,principal,token,expiresAt}`. These complete
sessions can be passed directly to createClient. Ordinary sessions last seven
days; sign in again with `login` from snapback4/auth when needed. Setup is
sequential and bounded (default five minutes, timeoutMs overrides it, max 100
accounts). Identity 429 refusals wait for retryAfterMs (Retry-After on older
servers), at most four attempts per account within the remaining setup budget.
SDK guest/signup/login share this policy (timeoutMs defaults to three minutes;
signal cancels a request or wait). Ambiguous writes never retry. Partial failure can leave accounts/grants: inspect or discard this
disposable deployment before retrying. Returned credentials never enter helper
logs. Print them only to a private operator destination, as above.

## Production refuses bulk setup

`deploy --staging` can classify only a new store. Existing unmarked stores are
production, and refuse relabelling. Classification stays with the store across
hot deploys, rollbacks and restarts; copying production into a directory named
staging does not change it. `deploy --check --staging` does not mark a store.
There is no promotion flag: deploy real production into its own fresh directory
and provision only intended users. `seedAccounts` and startServe's seedUsers
refuse production or dev before minting a token or creating any account. A
normal production startServe still supports operatorToken for explicit grants.

### E_STAGING

E_STAGING refuses production relabelling or bulk account setup on a non-staging
host. Choose a separate fresh `deploy --staging` store; use the explicit
first-admin recipe below for intended production users. Source edits and a
directory named staging never change a production store's classification.


### Account setup refusals

E_STAGING_INPUT means the account plan is invalid. Provide 1..100 accounts,
named roles, a declared mutation handle and a positive timeoutMs before setup.

E_STAGING_TIMEOUT means the setup deadline expired. Partial accounts/grants
may exist; inspect or discard the disposable deployment before retrying.

E_STAGING_SIGNUP means password signup refused or returned an invalid session.
Inspect the underlying refusal; partial accounts/grants may exist, so inspect
or discard the disposable deployment before retrying.

E_STAGING_PROVISION means the operator mutation refused or returned an invalid
reply. Repair its arguments and operator-only rules; partial accounts/grants
may exist, so inspect or discard the disposable deployment before retrying.

### Identity request deadline

E_IDENTITY_TIMEOUT is the internal sign-in deadline cause (SDK E_OFFLINE).
Signup outcome may be unknown: inspect before retrying. Increase timeoutMs to
allow server retryAfterMs waits. seedAccounts maps it to E_STAGING_TIMEOUT
with the partial-account recovery guidance above.

## Proof API (generated from source)

Every runtime and type export of `snapback4/proof` is listed below. Signatures, result fields and failure reasons are generated from TypeScript declarations and their @throws comments; qualification checks this table against the source. Playwright types such as Page and Locator come from playwright-core.

| Signature | Result / fields | Failure reasons |
|---|---|---|
| `bounded<T>(work: Promise<T>, ms: number, label: string): Promise<T>` | `Awaited<T>` | deadline expires; work rejection propagates. |
| `cleanup(...steps: Array<(() => unknown \| Promise<unknown>) \| undefined>): Promise<void>` | `void` | AggregateError after every cleanup was attempted; each step has a 10-second bound. |
| `cli(args: readonly string[], options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number \| null; signal: NodeJS.Signals \| null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. |
| `command(binary: string, args: readonly string[], options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number \| null; signal: NodeJS.Signals \| null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | spawn error or command deadline; nonzero exit is returned in code, not thrown. |
| `deploy(dir: string, schema: string, options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number \| null; signal: NodeJS.Signals \| null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | missing explicit saved source, CLI failure, spawn error or deadline. |
| `fillRN(locator: Locator, text: string): Promise<void>` | `void` | locator ambiguity, disabled/closed control, or Playwright deadline. |
| `frontPage(page: Page): Promise<Page>` | `Page` | page closed or browser activation failed. |
| `loadFixtures(directory: string, options: LoadFixturesOptions): Promise<FixtureRow[]>` | `Array of { table: string; row: Record<string, unknown> }` | E_PROOF_USAGE for missing url or invalid timeout; E_AUTH naming attempted credential; catalog, clock, filesystem, row JSON/table or E_FIXTURE_TIME validation failure. |
| `logsDir(options?: Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" \| "logsDir">): string` | `string` | None; returns the resolved explicit or cwd/logs path. |
| `portRange(env?: NodeJS.ProcessEnv): string` | `string` | partial, invalid or reversed PORT_LO/PORT_HI pair. |
| `renderedText(page: Page, text: string \| RegExp, options?: { within?: Locator; exact?: boolean; }): Locator` | `Locator` | None at construction; locator observation can refuse ambiguous or invalid selectors. |
| `rollback(dir: string, to: number \| "latest", options?: ProofOptions): Promise<CliResult>` | `{ code: number \| null; signal: NodeJS.Signals \| null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | invalid explicit release target, CLI failure, spawn error or deadline. |
| `sandboxUnavailable(error: unknown): boolean` | `false or true` | None; returns a boolean for recognized sandbox launch errors. |
| `seedAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" \| "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions>` | `StagingSessions` | E_STAGING_INPUT for invalid plans; E_STAGING for production/dev hosts; E_STAGING_TIMEOUT, E_STAGING_SIGNUP or E_STAGING_PROVISION for incomplete setup. Partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard the staging store before retrying. |
| `selectByValue(locator: Locator, value: string): Promise<void>` | `void` | locator ambiguity, unavailable option or Playwright deadline. |
| `startDev(options?: ProofOptions \| readonly string[]): Promise<DevHost>` | `{ personas: string[]; now: () => Promise<number>; url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` | invalid port range or conflicting memory flags; child exits, readiness deadline, PID/served identity mismatch, or unavailable dev clock. |
| `startServe(deployDir: string, options?: ServeOptions \| readonly string[]): Promise<ServeHost>` | `ServeHost` | E_PROOF_USAGE for missing deployment directory; child exit, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. stop() rejects bounded cleanup failure. |
| `startStatic(dir: string, options?: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost>` | `{ url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` | missing export/index or build script precondition; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. |
| `startVite(dir: string, options?: ProofOptions): Promise<ProofHost>` | `{ url: string; pid: number; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; }; stop: () => Promise<void> }` | missing Vite dependency/configuration; exhausted ports, readiness deadline or served identity mismatch. |
| `statusText(page: Page, text?: string \| RegExp): Locator` | `Locator` | None at construction; ambiguous text is refused when observed. |
| `stopChild(child: ChildProcess \| Pick<ProofHost, "url" \| "stop">, graceMs?: number): Promise<void>` | `void` | invalid handle/deadline, host stop failure or TERM/KILL reap deadline. |
| `testSource(source: string, options?: TestSourceOptions): Promise<TestSourceResult>` | `{ casesRun: number; casesSkipped: number; code: number \| null; signal: NodeJS.Signals \| null; stdout: string; stderr: string; logs: { stdout: string; stderr: string; } }` | missing source, CLI failure, invalid business-case summary or zero cases without fixtureOnly. |
| `waitForClock(page: Page, advancedNow: number, options?: { timeoutMs?: number; }): Promise<number>` | `number` | E_PROOF_USAGE for invalid advanced time; missing/disabled witness, signed-out client, page error or page-clock deadline. |
| `waitForFresh(locator: Locator, previous: string \| null, options?: number \| { within?: Locator; timeoutMs?: number; }): Promise<string>` | `string` | observation deadline with scoped locator, last text and visible headings; selector ambiguity is propagated. |
| `waitForServer<T>(read: () => Promise<T>, accepts: (value: T) => boolean, options?: { timeoutMs?: number; label?: string; }): Promise<T>` | `Awaited<T>` | deadline naming label and last value; read or predicate errors propagate. |
| `withBrowser<T>(options: LaunchOptions & Pick<ProofOptions, "cwd" \| "logsDir"> & { chromium?: BrowserType; }, proof: (context: BrowserContext) => Promise<T>): Promise<T>` | `Awaited<T>` | E_PROOF_BROWSER for missing playwright-core; invalid deadline, browser launch failure or callback error; bounded context/browser cleanup failure. |

      if (revoked.ok) {
        await endSession();
        await client.close();
      } else {

        if (sessionDead) await client.close();
      }

    },
    async signOut() {

      // Capture before endSession callbacks; track until adoption ends, including its resolved microtask.

      if (rotation) void rotation.then(refreshed => {

      }).catch(() => {});

    const revision = credentialRevision, epoch = queryEpoch;

    // ok:true and flow to the controller's refused state without polling.

    return { ok: false, why: json.denied ?? invalidResponse(response.status, "session refresh") };
  } catch (error) {
    return { ok: false, why: transportRefusal(error) };
  }
}

/** End every live session for the bearer token's principal. */
export async function logoutAll(url: string, token: string, doFetch: typeof fetch = defaultFetch): Promise<SessionRevocation> {
  try {
    const response = await doFetch(`${url.replace(/\/$/, "")}/auth/logout-all`, { method: "POST", headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } });
    const json = (await readResponse(response)) as { ok?: boolean; revoked?: number; denied?: Refusal };
    if (json.denied) return { ok: false, why: json.denied };
    if (response.ok && (json.ok === true || typeof json.revoked === "number")) return { ok: true, revoked: Number(json.revoked ?? 0) };
    return { ok: false, why: refused("session revocation was refused") };
  } catch (error) {
    return { ok: false, why: transportRefusal(error) };
  }
}

/** Where a device keeps its bearer session. The default is memory, so a web
 * page cannot read a long-lived token from browser storage. Pass a secure
 * platform store for continuity across starts. */
export interface SessionStore {
  get(key: string): Promise<string | null>;
  set(key: string, value: string): Promise<void>;
  delete(key: string): Promise<void>;
}

/** A session store that forgets when this JavaScript runtime exits. */
export function memorySessionStore(): SessionStore {
  const m = new Map<string, string>();
  return { async get(k) { return m.get(k) ?? null; }, async set(k, v) { m.set(k, v); }, async delete(k) { m.delete(k); } };
}

export const defaultSessionStore: SessionStore = memorySessionStore();

/** Structural session mirror, usable without DOM types. */
export interface StorageLike {
  getItem(key: string): string | null;
  setItem(key: string, value: string): void;
  removeItem(key: string): void;
}

/** Explicit browser persistence: coherent IndexedDB authority and a synchronous
 * localStorage mirror. Custom mirror adapters need a stable unique namespace.
 * Any script on this origin can read the token; opt in only when acceptable. */
export function localStorageSessionStore(storage?: StorageLike, authorityNamespace?: string): SessionStore {
  const target = () => {
    const found = storage ?? globalThis.localStorage;
    if (!found) throw new Error("localStorage is unavailable in this runtime");
    return found;
  };
  const store = browserAdmission() ? indexedDbSessionStore(target, authorityNamespace ?? (!storage || storage === globalThis.localStorage ? "default" : undefined)) : {
    async get(key) { return target().getItem(key); },
    async set(key, value) { target().setItem(key, value); },
    async delete(key) { target().removeItem(key); },
  } satisfies SessionStore;
  return synchronousSessionStore(store, key => target().getItem(key));
}

export const sessionKey = (url: string, readOnly = false) => storeKey("session", url.replace(/\/$/, "")) + (readOnly ? ".read" : "");

/** The session kept for a server, if it has not expired. */
export async function restoreSession(url: string, store: SessionStore = defaultSessionStore, now: () => number = Date.now, readOnly = false): Promise<Session | null> {
  const key = sessionKey(url, readOnly);
  const raw = await store.get(key);
  if (!raw || logoutEpoch(raw) !== null) return null;
  try {
    const session = JSON.parse(raw) as Session;
    if (isSession(session, now())) return session;
  } catch { /* Invalid JSON follows the same cleanup path as an expired session. */ }
  // A store may condition cleanup on the exact bytes returned by get. Let a
  // changed-snapshot or storage failure propagate instead of deleting twice.
  await store.delete(key);
  return null;
}

export async function keepSession(url: string, session: Session, store: SessionStore = defaultSessionStore, readOnly = false): Promise<void> {
  await store.set(sessionKey(url, readOnly), JSON.stringify(session));
}

export async function forgetSession(url: string, store: SessionStore = defaultSessionStore, readOnly = false): Promise<void> {
  await store.delete(sessionKey(url, readOnly));
}

/** Best-effort server logout. A network failure or timeout cannot confirm
 * revocation; callers must clear their local credentials independently. */
export async function logout(url: string, token: string, doFetch: typeof fetch = defaultFetch): Promise<void> {
  let timer!: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>;
  const deadline = new Promise<void>(resolve => {
    timer = setTimeout(resolve, 5_000);
    (timer as { unref?: () => void }).unref?.();
  });
  try {
    await Promise.race([doFetch(`${url.replace(/\/$/, "")}/auth/logout`, { method: "POST", headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } }), deadline]);
  } catch {
    // Offline: the token expires on its own.
  } finally { clearTimeout(timer); }
}

### K0a implementation note

K0a introduces the crate-private reference `Committer`, `Member` and `Kind`
in `snapback4-core/src/engine/committer.rs`. Each member runs in place and
opens its original transaction through the committer. Its body selects the
original commit point; explicit rollback and implicit drop remain distinct.
Post-commit continuations run synchronously in submission order. A
`Committed<T>` acknowledgement carries hooks across the adapter/engine
boundary for activation and reset, including ephemeral rebind and expiry
continuation clearing. There is no queue, batching, retry or persisted revision.

The sequenced producers in §2, including trusted seed deletion, now enter
that seam. Activation rebuild and Following launch retain their shared
activation transaction; `seed_sequences` remains a savepoint in seed's
member. The private time-clock pin also enters as a direct member. Mutation's
publication prelude stays in place; the evaluation Postgres `mutate_once`
path is unchanged. Host wakeups remain at their existing caller boundaries.

`crates/snapback4/tests/committer_ratchet.rs` shares the store-port ratchet's
lexer and permits only falling production opener counts outside the committer
and adapters. The base has 62 matching lines; K0a leaves 49 in 11 files.
The remaining K0b candidates cover identity/session operations, action/route
admission and lifecycle, work claims/settlement/leases/presence, action/effect
steps, asset intents/views/sweeps/receipt-only reports, job/schedule admission,
opening upgrades and development replay schedule metadata. Six of the 49
are authority snapshots conservatively counted because their handle can
write. Adapter-internal transactions are excluded, so their typed identity,
release and maintenance entry points also need K0b review. This is the first
slice of K0, not completion of the stream increment.

K0 and K1 are useful whatever S1a says. K1's read sets also feed increment
E (LLP 3000.009 §7.1).

**S1a's verdict (2026-10-20).** The predicted ceiling at 0.5 ms is 4,775
commits/s, bound by the execution stage at 32 connections (about 12 round
trips per execution). That is about 40× today's commit path, but 13.5% of
raw Postgres with 32 writers against the 25% × 2 the pre-registered rule
requires. So **K2–K5 wait**. First comes the rule's named fallback:

  function observeEpoch(epoch: string) {
    // A provisional owner does not replace the rollback candidate's duty to
    // observe the shared epoch. Fence every older scope before any callback.
    fenceScopes([...scopes].filter(scope => scope.valid && BigInt(epoch) > BigInt(scope.epoch)), new SessionEpochChanged());
  }
  function fence(scope: Scope, error: SessionEpochChanged) {
    fenceScopes([scope], error);
  }
  function fenceScopes(stale: Scope[], error: SessionEpochChanged) {
    for (const scope of stale) scope.valid = false;
    const affected = owner && stale.includes(owner) ? owner : undefined;
    const expected = revision;
    // Publish before raw retirement emits client callbacks, with every rollback
    // candidate already invalid. Auth and client observers share this boundary.
    if (affected && !closed) hide("restoring");
    for (const scope of stale) {
      scope.pause(); scope.stop(); scope.aborter.abort();
      if (scope.client) void scope.client.signOut().catch(() => {});
    }
    if (!affected || closed) return;
    void enqueue(async () => {
      if (owner !== affected) return;
      await release();
      if (revision === expected) publish("signed-out", null, error.why);
    }).catch(report);
  }
  async function open(session: Session, expected: number, epoch: string, restoring = false, openingSignOuts?: Record<string, number>): Promise<SignIn> {
    if (closed || revision !== expected) { revokeRetired(session.token); return inactive(); }
    const previous = owner, previousVisible = snapshot.state === "signed-in";
    hide("restoring", false);
    // Keep the prior runtime's rotations and pending-write custody alive until
    // commit. A failed replacement must not repair, revoke or detach it.
    // Every opener owns its persistence, including before it returns a Client.
    // Logout invalidates this guard synchronously, ahead of any network work.
    const scope: Scope = { valid: true, epoch, session, issued: new Set([session.token]), aborter: new AbortController(), stop() {}, pause() {} };
    const retire = (token: string) => revokeRetired(token, { principal: session.principal, epoch: scope.epoch });
    const cancelled = (): SignIn => {
      // A peer can fence this provisional scope before it becomes owner.
      // The still-current auth operation must finish cleanup/publication (or
      // retry restoration), rather than return with its view left restoring.
      if (!scope.valid && !closed && revision === expected) throw restoring ? new RestorationChanged() : new SessionEpochChanged();
      return inactive();
    };
    scopes.add(scope);
    if (options.device) refuseReadDevice(session);
    let restoreToken = restoring ? session.token : undefined;
    let preparing = true, committing = false;
    const scopedStore: SessionStore = {
      get: sessionStore.get,
      set: (key, value) => persist(() => storeLock(key, async () => {
        if (key !== custodyKey) { await storage.set(key, value); return; }
        const next = JSON.parse(value) as Session;
        const saved = await storage.get(key);
        let kept: Session | undefined;
        try { kept = saved ? JSON.parse(saved) : undefined; } catch {}
        if (!scope.valid || await readEpoch() !== epoch || (openingSignOuts &&
          (signOuts(saved)[session.principal] ?? 0) !== (openingSignOuts[session.principal] ?? 0))) {
          retire(next.token);
          const error = new SessionEpochChanged(); fence(scope, error);
          throw restoreToken !== undefined ? new RestorationChanged() : error;
        }
        if (restoreToken !== undefined && kept?.token !== restoreToken) {
          if (next.token !== scope.session.token && next.token !== kept?.token) retire(next.token);
          throw new RestorationChanged();
        }
        // Constructors can write/rotate several times. These saves are private
        // until their final successful opening; no durable account is displaced.
        if (preparing && !committing) { scope.session = next; return; }
        const replacing = preparing && !restoring;
        if (!replacing && kept?.token && kept.token !== scope.session.token && kept?.token !== next.token) {
          retire(next.token);
          const error = new SessionEpochChanged(); fence(scope, error);
          throw restoreToken !== undefined ? new RestorationChanged() : error;
        }
        if (replacing) await revocations.stage([...(kept?.token ? [kept.token] : []), ...revocations.pending()].filter(token => token !== next.token));
        const nextEpoch = replacing && kept?.token && kept.token !== next.token ? (BigInt(epoch) + 1n).toString() : epoch;
        // I2/I5: no separate epoch write and no fallible opening after this set.
        // Advancing the epoch already fences every opener that captured these
        // watermarks. Same-epoch rotations must keep their principal fences.
        if (kept?.token !== next.token || replacing || kept?.expiresAt !== next.expiresAt || kept?.capExpiresAt !== next.capExpiresAt) await storage.set(key, JSON.stringify({ ...next, snapback4Epoch: nextEpoch, snapback4Backend: url,
          ...(nextEpoch === epoch && Object.keys(signOuts(saved)).length ? { snapback4SignOuts: signOuts(saved) } : {}) }));
        lastOwned = { principal: next.principal, epoch: nextEpoch };
        epoch = nextEpoch; scope.epoch = nextEpoch; scope.session = next; scope.consumed = undefined;
        scope.issued.clear(); scope.issued.add(next.token);
        if (restoreToken !== undefined) restoreToken = next.token;
      })),
      delete: key => scope.valid ? (key === custodyKey ? removeSession(scope.session.token, restoreToken !== undefined) : sessionStore.delete(key)) : Promise.resolve(),
    };

    function authorized(): Promise<boolean> {
      // Memory/native session stores keep their original scheduling: their
// @ref LLP 3000.011.003.000#11-wave-11-runtime-upgrade-history — read installed release history after package-manager symlink replacement.
import { readFileSync, writeFileSync, mkdirSync, renameSync } from 'node:fs';
import { join, dirname } from 'node:path';
import { createRequire } from 'node:module';
import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
const version = value => /^\d+\.\d+\.\d+(?:-[0-9A-Za-z.-]+)?$/.test(value ?? '') ? value : undefined;
export function lockedVersion(text) {
  try {
    const lock = JSON.parse(text);
    const found = version(lock.packages?.['node_modules/snapback4']?.version ?? lock.dependencies?.snapback4?.version);
    if (found) return found;
  } catch { /* Bun lockfiles are JSONC; pnpm is YAML. */ }
  const lines = text.split('\n');
  const found = new Set();
  for (let i = 0; i < lines.length; i++) if (/^\s*['"]?snapback4['"]?:\s*$/.test(lines[i])) {
    for (const line of lines.slice(i + 1, i + 5)) {
      const resolved = /^\s*version:\s*(.+)$/.exec(line)?.[1]?.trim().replace(/^['"]|['"]$/g, '').split('(')[0];
      if (version(resolved)) found.add(resolved);
    }
  }
  for (const match of text.matchAll(/snapback4@([^\s"'/:()[\]{},]+)/g)) if (version(match[1])) found.add(match[1]);
  return found.size === 1 ? [...found][0] : undefined;
}
export function installedVersion(root) {
  try { const require = createRequire(join(root, 'package.json')); return version(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(dirname(dirname(require.resolve('snapback4/bin-path'))), 'package.json'), 'utf8')).version); } catch { /* Older copied trees may have no installed compiler. */ }
  return undefined;
}
export function previousVersion(root) {
  for (const name of ['runtime-version.json', 'check-surface.json', 'upgrade-version.json']) {
    try { const found = version(JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, '.snapback4', name), 'utf8')).version); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent or invalid */ }
  }
  try { const pkg = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(root, 'snapback/generated/package.json'), 'utf8')); const found = version(pkg.snapback4Version ?? pkg.version); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent */ }
  for (const name of ['package-lock.json', 'npm-shrinkwrap.json', 'bun.lock', 'pnpm-lock.yaml']) {
    try { const found = lockedVersion(readFileSync(join(root, name), 'utf8')); if (found) return found; } catch { /* absent */ }
  }
  return installedVersion(root);
}
export function acknowledgeVersion(root, version) {
  const directory = join(root, '.snapback4'); mkdirSync(directory, { recursive: true });
  const path = join(directory, 'runtime-version.json'), temporary = `${path}.${process.pid}.tmp`;
  writeFileSync(temporary, JSON.stringify({ version })); renameSync(temporary, path);
}
export function compareVersions(a, b) {
  const parts = v => { const i = v.indexOf('-'); return i < 0 ? [v, undefined] : [v.slice(0, i), v.slice(i + 1)]; };
  const [ac, ap] = parts(a), [bc, bp] = parts(b);
  const av = ac.split('.').map(Number), bv = bc.split('.').map(Number);
  for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) if (av[i] !== bv[i]) return av[i] - bv[i];
  if (ap === undefined || bp === undefined) return ap === bp ? 0 : ap === undefined ? 1 : -1;
  const aa = ap.split('.'), ba = bp.split('.');
  for (let i = 0; i < Math.max(aa.length, ba.length); i++) {
    if (aa[i] === ba[i]) continue;
    if (aa[i] === undefined || ba[i] === undefined) return aa[i] === undefined ? -1 : 1;
    const an = /^\d+$/.test(aa[i]), bn = /^\d+$/.test(ba[i]);
    if (an && bn) { const av = aa[i].replace(/^0+/, ''), bv = ba[i].replace(/^0+/, ''); if (av === bv) continue; return av.length - bv.length || (av < bv ? -1 : 1); }
    if (an !== bn) return an ? -1 : 1;
    return aa[i] < ba[i] ? -1 : 1;
  }
  return 0;
}
export function runtimeChanges(history, previous, installed) {
  if (!version(previous) || !version(installed) || compareVersions(previous, installed) >= 0) return '';
  let selected = false, out = '';
  for (const line of history.split('\n')) {
    if (line.startsWith('## ')) { const v = line.slice(3); selected = !!version(v) && compareVersions(v, previous) > 0 && compareVersions(v, installed) <= 0; }
    if (selected && line.startsWith('## ')) out += (out ? '\n' : '') + line + '\n\n';
    else if (selected && line.startsWith('- ')) out += line + '\n';
  }
  return out ? `runtime changes: ${previous} → ${installed}\n${out}History begins at 0.4.26; earlier changes are not recorded.\n` : '';
}

  const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, ['--input-type=module', '-e', script, root], { encoding: 'utf8' });
  if (result.error || result.status !== 0) throw Error('installed release history unavailable');
  return readFileSync(result.stdout.trim(), 'utf8');

        });
    }
    let why = reasons.join("; ");
    let why = if why.starts_with("the unmodified") { why } else { format!("the unmodified starter screen: {why}") };
    found.push(Leftover { path: app_path, why, rewrite: Some(app_text) });
    found.extend(client_leftover);

    fn finalize_effect_cancellation(&self, id: &str, now: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.require_serialized_head_fence("finalize effect cancellation")?;
        self.execute("UPDATE _outbox SET state = 'failed', error = 'the effect was cancelled; external effects are not undone', error_code = 'E_EFFECT_CANCELLED', settled_at = $2, lease_until = NULL, claimed_by = NULL, lease_digest = NULL, next_at = NULL WHERE id = $1", &[&id, &now])?;
        Ok(())
    }
    fn cancel_effect(&self, id: &str, now: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        self.require_serialized_head_fence("cancel effect")?;
        self.execute("UPDATE _outbox SET state = 'failed', cancelled = true, error = 'the effect was cancelled; external effects are not undone', error_code = 'E_EFFECT_CANCELLED', settled_at = $2, lease_until = NULL, claimed_by = NULL, lease_digest = NULL, next_at = NULL WHERE id = $1", &[&id, &now])?;
        Ok(())
    }
    fn shift_worker_leases(&self, milliseconds: i64, before: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let (bound, end) = crate::engine::effects::lease_shift_bounds(milliseconds);
        self.require_serialized_head_fence("shift worker leases")?;
        // SQLite LIKE folds ASCII only. C-collated ILIKE preserves that marker rule.
        self.execute("UPDATE _sessions SET expires_at = CASE WHEN ($1::bigint >= 0 AND expires_at > $2) OR ($1::bigint < 0 AND expires_at < $2) THEN $3 ELSE expires_at + $1 END WHERE expires_at > $4 AND COALESCE(family,token_digest) IN (SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'worker:session-family' UNION SELECT substr(key,15) FROM _meta WHERE key ILIKE 'worker:family:%' COLLATE \"C\")", &[&milliseconds, &bound, &end, &before])?;
        self.execute("UPDATE _outbox SET lease_until = CASE WHEN ($1::bigint >= 0 AND lease_until > $2) OR ($1::bigint < 0 AND lease_until < $2) THEN $3 ELSE lease_until + $1 END WHERE state = 'running' AND lease_until IS NOT NULL", &[&milliseconds, &bound, &end])?;
        self.execute("UPDATE _requests SET lease_until = CASE WHEN family IS NULL OR principal_generation IS NULL OR admitted_expires_at IS NULL THEN CASE WHEN ($1::bigint >= 0 AND lease_until > $2) OR ($1::bigint < 0 AND lease_until < $2) THEN $3 ELSE lease_until + $1 END ELSE LEAST(CASE WHEN ($1::bigint >= 0 AND lease_until > $2) OR ($1::bigint < 0 AND lease_until < $2) THEN $3 ELSE lease_until + $1 END, COALESCE((SELECT MAX(s.expires_at) FROM _sessions s WHERE s.principal = _requests.principal AND COALESCE(s.family,s.token_digest) = _requests.family), lease_until)) END WHERE state = 'running' AND lease_until IS NOT NULL", &[&milliseconds, &bound, &end])?;

        Ok(())
    }
    fn expired_effects(&self, now: i64, limit: u64) -> Result<Vec<EffectRun>, RuntimeError> {
        self.query(runs!("WHERE state IN ('queued','running') AND (deadline <= $1 OR (state = 'running' AND lease_until < $1)) ORDER BY seq, id LIMIT $2"), &[&now, &(limit.min(i64::MAX as u64) as i64)])?.into_iter().map(read_run).collect()
    }

    }

        Ok(())
    }

    fn shift_worker_leases(&self, milliseconds: i64, before: i64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let (bound, end) = crate::engine::effects::lease_shift_bounds(milliseconds);
        let supervised: bool = self.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _meta WHERE key = 'worker:session-family' OR key LIKE 'worker:family:%')", [], |row| row.get(0))?;

        self.conn().execute("UPDATE _sessions SET expires_at = CASE WHEN (?1 >= 0 AND expires_at > ?2) OR (?1 < 0 AND expires_at < ?2) THEN ?3 ELSE expires_at + ?1 END WHERE expires_at > ?4 AND COALESCE(family, token_digest) IN (SELECT value FROM _meta WHERE key = 'worker:session-family' UNION SELECT substr(key, 15) FROM _meta WHERE key LIKE 'worker:family:%')", [milliseconds, bound, end, before])?;

        self.conn().execute("UPDATE _requests SET lease_until = CASE WHEN family IS NULL OR principal_generation IS NULL OR admitted_expires_at IS NULL THEN CASE WHEN (?1 >= 0 AND lease_until > ?2) OR (?1 < 0 AND lease_until < ?2) THEN ?3 ELSE lease_until + ?1 END ELSE MIN(CASE WHEN (?1 >= 0 AND lease_until > ?2) OR (?1 < 0 AND lease_until < ?2) THEN ?3 ELSE lease_until + ?1 END, COALESCE((SELECT MAX(s.expires_at) FROM _sessions s WHERE s.principal = _requests.principal AND COALESCE(s.family, s.token_digest) = _requests.family), lease_until)) END WHERE state = 'running' AND lease_until IS NOT NULL", [milliseconds, bound, end])?;

        Ok(())
    }

    fn expired_effects(&self, now: i64, limit: u64) -> Result<Vec<EffectRun>, RuntimeError> {
        let limit = limit.min(i64::MAX as u64) as i64;

        .query_map([now, limit], read_run)?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?)
    }

        Ok(self.conn().prepare(&format!("SELECT {COLUMNS} FROM _outbox WHERE state IN ('queued', 'running') AND (deadline <= ?1 OR (state = 'running' AND lease_until < ?1)) ORDER BY seq, id"))?
        .query_map([now], read_run)?.collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?)
    }

codex
The held-poll gap reaches the client’s expiry handler: a stream-final clock jump can sign out a read client whose server session was preserved. I also found separate issues in timing-save ordering and hidden-text aggregation. I’m collecting exact line references; the ongoing verification runs have not affected these findings.

codex
Three material findings remain for commit `70e50d228d2daaa1273d6f7a40ae85be1a33ca9c`:

1. **[P1] Streaming polls bypass session-timing reconciliation.** [clock.ts:104](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/clock.ts:104) registers heartbeat responses for final clock sampling, but [serve_changes.rs:254](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve_changes.rs:254) emits only final time/revision, without preserved expiry/cap or timing-unavailable evidence. An idle observed read client can therefore receive a preserving staging jump through `/changes`, sample beyond its old expiry, and sign itself out while its server credential remains live. Reconcile matching-bearer timing from the final frame before sampling; suppress that sample when authoritative timing is unavailable.

2. **[P2] Local timing persistence does not precede clock publication.** [local.ts:391](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/local.ts:391) queues the credential save and swallows its failure, while [clock.ts:103](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/clock.ts:103) immediately continues into sampling, durable clock persistence, and listeners. If the session save fails while the device clock save succeeds, offline reopening compares stale expiry against the advanced clock and deletes a still-live preserved session. Await fenced credential persistence before publishing/persisting the clock jump, with explicit failure handling.

3. **[P2] Invisible descendant text can become visible ancestor evidence.** [proof-text.ts:54](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b22-dx6/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-text.ts:54) returns accumulated text even when the element fails its visibility check. A visible container containing only a zero-size, `overflow:hidden` child reading “Submitted” consequently matches `renderedText(page, "Submitted")`; `waitForFresh` can report success without visible status text. Exclude text from non-rendered/clipped subtrees when aggregating ancestors, while retaining composed labels and layoutless wrappers.

This was a read-only source review. No builds/tests ran, and ongoing verification was treated as neither a defect nor a pass.
